Branch `feature/legacy-rme-pdf-1a-schema-permission-date-rules` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline SATUSEHAT-4D merge `3cd6452`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-pdf-1a-schema-permission-date-rules.md`; ADR `docs/adr/0008-legacy-rme-pdf-archive-foundation.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/90-legacy-rme-pdf-archive.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml`. **Foundation for "Import Arsip RME Lama Pasien" — the PDF a Super Admin will later upload is the patient's HISTORICAL RME data, not a template and not a live-workflow RME.** Operated from **Master Data → Master Data RME → Import Arsip RME Lama**. **Scope 1A = schema, permissions, policies, repositories, date-rule domain, audit foundation, config, tests ONLY.** Deliberately NOT shipped: PDF conversion/Poppler/Imagick/OCR/canvas/PDF.js, upload runtime, annotation, thumbnails, publish runtime, patient-timeline integration, doctor viewer, legacy print/PDF — and **no route, controller or Blade view exists in 1A** (an endpoint was not added merely for coverage; services are tested directly). Feature flag `rme.legacy_pdf_archive` (`FEATURE_RME_LEGACY_PDF_ARCHIVE`, risk `high`) **defaults OFF**, resolved via `LegacyRmeFeatureGuard`→`FeatureFlagService` (never `config('feature_flags…')` — dots break traversal). New bounded context `App\Modules\LegacyRme` (Interfaces/Models/Policies/Repositories/Services/Support; no module provider — bindings + policies go in `RepositoryServiceProvider`'s `private array $repositories`/`$policies`).

**Migrations (additive only — `migrate`, never `migrate:fresh`/`db:wipe`):** `2026_08_06_100001..4` create `stg_rme_legacy_imports`, `stg_rme_legacy_import_pages`, `trx_rme_legacy_records`, `trx_rme_legacy_record_pages`. Legacy records are deliberately NOT stored in `trx_medical_records` (which requires a NOT NULL + UNIQUE `clinic_visit_id` and drives the visit→cashier workflow) — a legacy RME has **no `clinic_visit_id` and never creates a fake visit**. Constraints: `UNIQUE(uuid)`, `UNIQUE(legacy_import_id,page_number)`, `UNIQUE(rme_legacy_record_id,page_number)`, **`UNIQUE(source_import_id)`** (one staging batch → at most one record, publishing idempotent), indexes on `(patient_id,rme_date)`, `(patient_id,status)`, `source_pdf_sha256`, `normalized_content_hash`. `source_pdf_sha256` is intentionally **not globally unique** (cross-patient duplicate investigation + VOID-then-reimport need repeats). Staging soft-deletes; **published tables carry NO soft delete** — immutability is the contract. FK posture: patient/branch RESTRICT/NULL (never cascade clinical rows), staging pages cascade with their batch, published pages RESTRICT (evidence).

**Domain contract (permanent, mirrored in rule 90):** (1) legacy RME = archive of a patient's OLD RME data from a historical PDF; (2) never creates a clinic visit, invoice, payment, consent, odontogram, `LabCaseCandidate`, `LabOrder` or SATUSEHAT candidate, never changes visit status, never enters visit/revenue KPI; (3) the date is **chosen manually** from what the document shows — never upload time / `created_at` / file date / PDF metadata / OCR — stored as `selected_rme_date` (staging) / `rme_date` (published), separate from `uploaded_at`/`published_at`; (4) **`legacy date < earliest_native_rme_date` STRICTLY** (equal = rejected, since equal is exactly the overlap case) **when a native RME exists — widened by FIX-ROLL2-1 to every date the document represents (`latest < earliest native RME`)**, plus `legacy date < today` (**today is rejected** — an archive is historical) and `patient birth date <= legacy date` (**equal to the birth date is accepted**); `mst_patients.date_of_birth` is nullable so a missing value **skips** that rule and is never invented; (5) **SUPERSEDED by LEGACY-RME-PDF-FIX-ROLL2-1** — 1A refused a patient with no native RME (`PATIENT_HAS_NO_NATIVE_RME`); such a patient is now ELIGIBLE (the native RME is a bound, not a prerequisite) and a native encounter is never manufactured to unlock an import; (6) an existing legacy row (staged/published/**VOID**) is never native RME and never becomes the comparison point; (7) a published record is **immutable** — correction is **VOID with a reason + fresh import**, never an in-place edit or hard delete.

**Canonical clinical date:** `earliest_native_rme_date` derives from **`trx_clinic_visits.visit_date`** via the visit that owns the medical record. `trx_medical_records` has **no clinical date column at all**. Rejected (each a real trap): `created_at` (Sprint 59 removed the finalize lock + Sprint 64.0 lets a doctor open a sheet from a later visit → row written long after the encounter), `finalized_at` (nullable workflow timestamp), `canonical_visit_id` (a cache — its own migration says the resolver is the live source of truth), `mst_patients.registered_at` (administrative). **Single source of truth = `App\Modules\LegacyRme\Services\PatientEarliestNativeRmeDateResolver`** backed by new `ClinicVisitRepository::earliestVisitWithMedicalRecordForPatient(int $patientId)` (+ interface) — no controller/FormRequest/policy/view/test may re-derive it. Excludes cancelled visits, visits without an MR, soft-deleted visits/records, and every legacy row; orders `(visit_date, id)` for PG/SQLite stability. **Deliberately NOT branch-scoped** (diverges from `PatientRmWorkspaceResolver::resolveCanonicalVisit()` on purpose, pinned by a test): a narrower scan could only move the bound LATER and admit an overlapping document — access control is a separate concern. **Timezone:** "today" uses `legacy_rme.dates.clinical_timezone` (defaults to the app timezone — the same wall clock `ClinicVisitService` uses when stamping `visit_date` via `Carbon::today()`), and `visit_date` is treated as an opaque calendar date and never shifted; realigning the setting moves the boundary by at most one calendar day.

**Rules service:** `LegacyRmeDateRuleService` with stable codes (`PATIENT_HAS_NO_NATIVE_RME` **removed by FIX-ROLL2-1**; `LEGACY_DATE_RANGE_INVALID` added) `LEGACY_DATE_NOT_BEFORE_NATIVE_RME`, `LEGACY_DATE_IN_FUTURE` (covers today + future), `LEGACY_DATE_BEFORE_PATIENT_BIRTH`, `LEGACY_DATE_INVALID`; `evaluate()` returns an immutable `LegacyRmeDateRuleResult` (branch on the CODE, never the message), `assert()` throws `ValidationException`, `snapshotCutoff()` produces the server-side `earliest_native_rme_date_snapshot` — **the cutoff is always recomputed server-side; a client-supplied snapshot is never trusted**. The rule context is PII-free by construction (dates + `patient_id` only).

**Status domain:** no native PHP `enum` exists anywhere in `app/`, so vocabularies are final Support classes + model constants — `LegacyRmeImportStatus` (`DRAFT→UPLOADED→QUEUED→PROCESSING→READY_FOR_REVIEW→REVIEWED→PUBLISHED` + `FAILED`/`CANCELLED`, explicit `TRANSITIONS`, terminal = PUBLISHED/CANCELLED), `LegacyRmeImportPageStatus` (fail-closed: default `PENDING`, only `READY` is publishable), `LegacyRmeRecordStatus` (`PUBLISHED→VOID` only).

**RBAC (5 new permissions, granted to NO operational role):** `view_legacy_rme_imports`, `create_legacy_rme_imports`, `review_legacy_rme_imports`, `publish_legacy_rme_imports`, `void_legacy_rme_imports` — Super Admin (the designated Master Data RME operator) holds them via the existing RoleSeeder `'*'` grant + the single global `Gate::before`; classified into the existing `rme` `PermissionGroupingService` group. Policies `LegacyRmeImportPolicy` (viewAny/view/create/review/publish/cancel) + `LegacyRmeRecordPolicy` (viewAny/view/void; **`update`/`delete` hard-wired `false`**) registered in `RepositoryServiceProvider::$policies` (`private`, not `protected` — copying stock Laravel registers nothing; there is no `AuthServiceProvider`). No policy `before()` hook (it would shadow the single global bypass). Branch isolation via `LegacyRmeWorkspaceScope` (governance tier = all RME-enabled branches, others pinned to `BranchContext::forUser`, **empty scope = deny-all**); **`origin_branch_id` is provenance metadata, never authorization**, and the branch is never read from the request. Repositories take the resolved branch-id list as the first parameter and fail closed (`whereRaw('1 = 0')`) on an empty scope.

**Audit:** reuses the shared `sys_audit_logs` trail through `AuditLogService` (no new audit table). `LegacyRmeAuditService` adds the payload policy the shared service lacks — every payload is filtered against `LegacyRmeAuditEvent::ALLOWED_METADATA_KEYS` and reduced to length-bounded scalars. Events `LEGACY_RME_IMPORT_CREATED`/`DATE_SELECTED`/`PUBLISH_REJECTED`/`DUPLICATE_DETECTED`/`PUBLISHED`/`VOIDED`. Never persisted: patient name, KTP/NIK, clinical content, base64, raw PDF, absolute path.

**Test-infra gotcha:** module models must declare `protected static function newFactory()` — Laravel otherwise resolves `Database\Factories\Modules\LegacyRme\Models\…Factory` and fails (all factories live in `database/factories/`, `Database\Factories\`). Tests: `tests/Feature/LegacyRme/{LegacyRmeSchemaTest,LegacyRmeFoundationTest,PatientEarliestNativeRmeDateResolverTest,LegacyRmeDateRuleServiceTest,LegacyRmePermissionTest,LegacyRmeNonRegressionTest}` (73). The suite is wired into the **CI critical filter** (`.github/workflows/foundation-evidence-gates.yml` — appended `|LegacyRme|PatientEarliestNativeRmeDateResolver`) because the CICD-CTRL-1 Selective Module Gate branches only on Inventory/Lab/Ui/Permission and `run_rme_tests` is consumed by no job. Deploy: `php artisan migrate --force` (4 additive tables) + `db:seed --class=PermissionSeeder --force` + `permission:cache-reset`; keep `FEATURE_RME_LEGACY_PDF_ARCHIVE` false. **Next: LEGACY-RME-PDF-1B** — upload runtime, page rendering, review UI, publish workflow, private-file serving, timeline integration and the doctor viewer, all behind the same flag.

## LEGACY-RME-PDF-1B — Private Upload Runtime, PDF Validation, Queue Processing & Page Rendering (2026-08-06)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-pdf-1b-private-upload-rendering` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline LEGACY-RME-PDF-1A merge `0b37ce4` / GO tag `legacy-rme-pdf-1a-schema-permission-date-rules-go`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-pdf-1b-private-upload-rendering.md`; ADR amendment in `docs/adr/0008-legacy-rme-pdf-archive-foundation.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/90-legacy-rme-pdf-archive.mdc` (rules 21–35). Implements the intake runtime: **Super Admin → Master Data RME → Impor Arsip RME Lama → pick patient (Nomor RM) → choose the legacy date MANUALLY → upload PDF → validate → private storage → QUEUE → `pdfinfo` inspect → `pdftoppm` rasterize + thumbnails → page rows → `READY_FOR_REVIEW`.** **NO migration** (1A already shipped every column). **No new permission** — the five 1A permissions are the boundary. `FEATURE_RME_LEGACY_PDF_ARCHIVE` stays **false**.

**Deliberately NOT in 1B:** review approval, publish, promotion into `trx_rme_legacy_records`, patient timeline, doctor viewer, legacy print, annotation/pen/eraser/canvas, OCR, automatic date extraction, VOID runtime. **There is no publish route** (asserted by test). `READY_FOR_REVIEW` is the furthest a document goes.

**Private storage:** new disk `legacy_rme_private` (`driver local`, root `storage_path('app/legacy-rme-private')`, **`serve => false`**, `visibility private`, `throw true`). Rooted **outside** the `local` disk root on purpose: Laravel registers a `storage.{disk}` route for a local disk with `serve => true`, so anything under `storage/app/private` is in principle addressable by a signed framework URL — clinical pages must not be. `LegacyRmeStorageService::assertPrivateDisk()` refuses a disk that is forbidden/public/carries a `url`/has `serve` enabled. Paths are opaque: `rme-legacy/imports/p{patient_id}/{import_uuid}/{source,pages,thumbnails,processing}` — never a name/KTP/phone/diagnosis, and the uploaded filename is a sanitized display label only, never part of a path.

**PDF pipeline:** contracts `LegacyRmePdfInspectorInterface` → `PopplerLegacyRmePdfInspector`, `LegacyRmePdfRasterizerInterface` → `PopplerLegacyRmePdfRasterizer`, `LegacyRmeMalwareScannerInterface` → `ConfiguredLegacyRmeMalwareScanner` (OFF by default; when disabled it reports "not scanned", **never** "passed"), plus `Fake*` doubles so no test needs Poppler. `LegacyRmeProcessRunner` is the only place a binary runs — always an **argument array** through Symfony `Process` (execve, no shell), explicit timeout, binary from config; stderr/exit code go to the log only, never onto the exception (whose message is rendered AND persisted to `failure_message`). **Thumbnails use Poppler `-scale-to`, not GD/Imagick** — the local dev machine has no GD and an image dep would make rendering environment-dependent; they are PNG, and `getimagesize()` (core, no extension) validates pages. **`pdftoppm` zero-pads its suffix to the page-count width** (9 pages → `-1`, 10 → `-01`), so output is always discovered by scanning the dir and parsing the suffix — never by assuming a filename (real-Poppler test covers the 12-page case). Stable codes in `LegacyRmePdfFailure` (22 of them); callers branch on the **code**, never the message.

**Upload order:** feature flag → 1A date rules (never re-derived) → origin-branch validation → structural validation + server-side SHA-256 → duplicate precheck → malware scan → store private PDF → `DB::transaction` create staging row → **after commit** dispatch. The file is written before the transaction (a filesystem write cannot join one); a failed transaction deletes the orphan (tested). **The malware scan runs at upload, not in the job** — infected bytes are then never stored at all, and re-scanning immutable bytes on retry adds no security.

**Duplicate policy (server-computed SHA-256; filename irrelevant both ways):** PUBLISHED record → block (same or other patient); **VOID record → allow** (1A defines correction as "VOID plus a fresh import", so blocking would break the documented path — the collision is audited); active staging → block; FAILED same-patient → block and point at **Retry** (no second staging row); FAILED/CANCELLED **other** patient → allow (the mis-assignment fix must stay possible).

**Queue:** `ProcessLegacyRmePdfImport` extends ENT-5 `EnterpriseQueueJob`, `ShouldBeUnique`, queue `legacy-rme-documents`, carries **only the import id**. Status claim is one locked transaction (duplicate delivery = no-op); stale output + stale page rows cleared before rendering; pages upsert on `UNIQUE(legacy_import_id,page_number)`; temp dir removed in `finally`. **Queue retries do not drive domain failure** — a failure lands `FAILED` with a stable code and stops, and the operator's explicit Retry (`FAILED → QUEUED`, legal in the 1A map) is the recovery path; `failed()` covers a worker that died before recording anything. **No `READY_FOR_REVIEW → QUEUED` edge was invented** (nor `FAILED` abused to fake one) — a rendered document is corrected by cancelling and re-uploading. A stale `PROCESSING` (worker died) becomes reclaimable after 2× timeout + margin.

**HTTP:** 9 routes `settings.rme.legacy-imports.{index,create,store,show,status,source,pages.show,retry,cancel}` under `/settings/rme/legacy-imports`. **Deliberately NOT nested inside the `manage patients` group** — 1A defines the five legacy permissions as *the* boundary, and inheriting a second requirement would make them insufficient alone (a view-only holder opening the index is asserted). Three independent gates per action: route `permission:` middleware, controller feature-flag check (**disabled ⇒ 404**, so the surface reveals nothing), and the policy (per-row branch scope; new abilities `retry` + `viewFile`). `{import}` is a plain integer resolved through `findByIdInBranches()` with the caller's scope — **out-of-scope is 404, not 403**, so ids cannot be probed; a page is looked up **through** its import so a page number cannot cross imports. File responses: explicit Content-Type, `nosniff`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`, generic download name (path and original filename never echoed).

**Security-review outcome:** two candidate findings were raised and both filtered to confidence 3 (below the ≥8 bar) — an equivalent, broader patient enumeration already ships via `settings/patients/{patient}/edit` (which even renders full KTP, for more roles), and the `origin_branch_id` issue is unreachable while only Super Admin holds the permission. **Both cheap fixes were applied anyway** because 1C will delegate these permissions: (1) `resolveOriginBranch()` now validates against the **uploader's own scope** and anchors a scoped operator's branchless upload to their own branch — the 1A claim that `origin_branch_id` is "provenance, never authorization" was **factually wrong** (repository listings filter on it, the policy scopes on it), and rule 14 has been corrected; (2) `assertPrivateDisk()` also refuses a disk with `serve` enabled; (3) patient selection by surrogate key goes through `LegacyRmePatientLookupService::findSelectable()`, applying the same doctor patient scope as the canonical Nomor-RM search, on **both** `create()` and `store()`.

**Test-infra:** new Pest helpers in `tests/Pest.php` — `legacyRmePdfBytes()` builds a structurally valid multi-page PDF in pure PHP (correct xref; no fixture binary committed, no clinical document used), `legacyRmePdfUpload()`, `legacyRmeNativeVisit()`, and `legacyRmeArchiveFlag()` (**the flag key contains a dot, so it is a literal array key — dot-notation config cannot reach it; the whole `feature_flags.flags` array must be rewritten**). Role-user HTTP tests need `withoutMiddleware(EnsureRmeOnlineContext::class)` (class lives in `App\Modules\RmeOnlineContext\Middleware`). `BranchContext` falls back to the first active RME-enabled branch, so branch-scope tests must pin `users.branch_id` explicitly or they pass for the wrong reason. **The 1A assertion "registers no HTTP route for the legacy RME archive" was replaced, not left to pass by accident** — its substring filters (`legacy-rme`/`legacy_rme`/`rme-legacy`) would not have matched the new `legacy-imports` naming, so it would have kept "passing" while the surface existed; it now asserts the exact staging route set **and** that `publish`/`review`/`approve`/`void` still have no endpoint.

**Config:** `config/legacy_rme.php` gains a `processing` block keeping the 1A `LEGACY_RME_*` env family (not a second `LEGACY_RME_PDF_*` family): `LEGACY_RME_DISK` (now `legacy_rme_private`), `_DPI` 180, `_THUMBNAIL_MAX_EDGE` 320, `_PROCESS_TIMEOUT` 180, `_MAX_PAGE_PIXELS` 40M, `_MAX_PAGE_DIMENSION_PT` 20000, `_MAX_RENDER_BYTES` 200MiB, `_QUEUE` `legacy-rme-documents`, `_TRIES` 3, `_MALWARE_SCAN` false. Tests: 7 suites, **179 passed / 551 assertions**; native RME regression 158 passed; permission/sidebar 42 passed. **Runtime dependency: `poppler-utils` (`pdfinfo`, `pdftoppm`)** on any host that processes documents; the real-Poppler suite **skips** (never silently passes) when absent. The `legacy-rme-documents` queue must be served by the managed worker before the flag is enabled. Deploy: **no migration, no seeder**. GO tag on merge: `legacy-rme-pdf-1b-private-upload-rendering-go`. **Next: LEGACY-RME-PDF-1C** — review approval, publish into `trx_rme_legacy_records`, patient-timeline integration, doctor viewer, legacy print.

## CICD-CTRL-3 — Dedicated Self-Hosted CI Runner (2026-08-07)

Branch `feature/cicd-ctrl-3-dedicated-self-hosted-ci-runner` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ `0b37ce4`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/cicd-ctrl-3-dedicated-self-hosted-ci-runner.md`; runbook `docs/runbooks/self-hosted-runner.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/91-dedicated-self-hosted-ci-runner.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (INFRA_RELEASE). **Status: `IMPLEMENTED / RUNNER OPERATIONAL / EQUIVALENCE PROVEN` — `WATCH — PRE-EXISTING AUTHORITATIVE CI FAILURES`. PR #267 NOT merged, NO GO tag.** **No migration, no permission, no product runtime change, no deploy.**

**Runner (operational).** `daengtisia-ci-01` on `aishrunner` (Dell Inspiron 14-3467 laptop, **i3-7020U 2c/4t**, 14 GiB, ~211 GB free — matches the approved spec; tailnet `100.74.126.71`, ssh alias `daengtisia-runner`, user `runner_dms`, dedicated key `~/.ssh/daengtisia_runner_ed25519`, production VPS key deliberately NOT reused). Online with labels `self-hosted,Linux,X64,daengtisia-ci`; systemd service enabled+active as **`github-runner` (uid 1001), never root**, zero interactive `run.sh`. **Ubuntu 26.04 kept — no OS reinstall.** `github-runner` had been in **both the `docker` and `sudo` groups** (root-equivalent); nothing depended on them and both were **removed** → `github-runner users`.

**Authoritative runtime is containerised, never the host.** Host ships only PHP 8.5, the gate pins **PHP 8.3** (all six `setup-php` blocks). So every php/composer/artisan call goes through `scripts/ci/self-hosted-php.sh` → **rootless Podman** (5.7.0, user socket, no Docker) running a **digest-pinned** image from `.github/ci-runtime/Containerfile.php83` (PHP 8.3.33 + the gate's exact extension set + Poppler; build FAILS if any is missing). `--userns=keep-id` ⇒ container writes owned by `github-runner`, **no root-owned residue**. **Gotchas:** the official `php:8.3-cli` defaults to `memory_limit=128M` and Pest OOM'd before running a single test (build now fails unless `-1`); Poppler must be in the image or LegacyRme's suite silently SKIPS; `ondrej/php` has **no `resolute` build** (HTTP 404) so 8.3 is unobtainable natively on 26.04.

**CI database pinned to `postgres:16`.** The host's PostgreSQL 18 caused **7 self-hosted-only failures** (`SQLSTATE[25P02]` cascades from `Dq1AuditService`). Canonical CI DB is now a pinned `postgres:16` (16.14) under rootless Podman as systemd **`ci-pg16.service`** (Quadlet), **host-networked** on **`127.0.0.1:5433` only** — `Network=host` avoids the `rootlessport` userspace proxy on principle, but note that a published-port configuration measured **no slower** in practice, so no performance claim is made for this choice. Host PG 18 on 5432 left untouched (co-tenant). Health check now FAILS on a major-version mismatch. Production also runs PG 16.14, so the host's 18 was the outlier.

**RUNNER RESULT EQUIVALENCE = PASS.** Same commit, same PHP 8.3, same PG 16, same filter, sequential both sides, strict exit both sides: GitHub-hosted `62 failed / 263 warnings / 1106 assertions` vs self-hosted+PG16 **identical**; failing-test-name sets **empty diff both directions** (61 distinct each).

**APPLICATION TEST GATE = RED (pre-existing, NOT this sprint).** Strict exit propagation (`shell: bash` → `-eo pipefail` + `PIPESTATUS` on **10 gate steps across BOTH runners**) exposed long-hidden debt: (A) **62 Vite-manifest failures** — `critical_test_gate` installs Composer only, never `npm ci`/`npm run build`, so `public/build/manifest.json` is absent and every test rendering `layouts/app.blade.php` throws; (B) **3 files failing `pint --test`** (`routes/web.php`, `Satusehat4dMultiBranchMatrixTest`, `StressSeedFoundationCommand`). **Proof they pre-date the sprint:** run `30189642130` (2026-07-26, base branch, GitHub-hosted, 11 days before this branch existed) had the **identical** 62-failure/61-distinct set and CI reported it **`success`**. Handed to **CICD-FIX-1** (`docs/sprints/cicd-fix-1-vite-manifest-test-environment-recovery.md`) — root-cause analysis required, `npm build` NOT assumed to be the answer. **Trap:** local `pint --dirty --test` checks only changed files; CI runs the whole repo.

**Defect found by running, not reading:** `release_safety_gate` depended only on `critical_test_gate`, and since exactly one variant runs while the other is *skipped*, GitHub skipped NSF-9 and transitively NSF-10 whenever routing went self-hosted. Both now depend on **both** variants and require one to have genuinely succeeded; guarded by test + scanner.

**Routing.** `classify` (always GitHub-hosted) resolves `runner_mode`: dispatch input → repo variable `CI_RUNNER_MODE` → **`github-hosted`** (fail-safe). Two variants share the check name `NSF-R011 Critical Test Gate` with mutually exclusive `if`s and an identical filter. A test proves **exactly one runs across all 15** mode × event × fork combinations; **fork PRs are redirected to GitHub-hosted, never skipped**; a runner outage **queues** the gate (no automatic failover).

**Critical-gate duration (both sides identical work — PHP 8.3, PG 16, same filter, sequential, same 62-failure result):** GitHub-hosted **1347s** vs aishrunner **1808s** ⇒ **aishrunner is 1.34× SLOWER** for CPU-bound execution (expected on a 2-core i3-7020U). **RETRACTED:** the earlier "307s on the runner" figure is INVALID — it was measured on the host PG 18 where governance tests short-circuited on aborted transactions (`fg1 ci check` 0.21s there vs 68.37s on GitHub-hosted), i.e. the suite did less work. The DB is NOT the bottleneck: a direct probe shows the PG16 container marginally *faster* than host PG18 (bulk 84ms vs 95ms, connect+commit 459ms vs 632ms) with identical settings. Queue wait / assignment delay / cache benefit are `N/A — not measured`.

**Benchmark (hardware capacity evidence only — `--parallel` NOT enabled on the gate):** 1/2/3 Pest workers → 326s / 206s / 162s (1.00×/1.58×/2.01×), zero swap, identical results at every count. **THERMAL METRIC = UNRELIABLE** (probe returned a static 25.0 °C; no no-throttling claim). **VPS is production** (`srv1730088`, `APP_ENV=pilot`, real clinical data, PHP 8.5.8) → **never a CI runner**; any VPS figure is `SOURCE = HISTORICAL EVIDENCE`; deployment timing is `PRODUCTION DEPLOYMENT PERFORMANCE`, never CI performance.

**Closure blocked on CICD-FIX-1.** Then: authoritative CI on the exact candidate → outage-queueing validation → final timing comparison → merge PR #267 → post-merge runner validation → **revoke the temporary `NOPASSWD` entry** (`/etc/sudoers.d/99-cicd-ctrl-3-temp`) → cleanup → immutable GO tag. Temporary sudo and all runtime evidence are **deliberately preserved** until then so the environment stays reproducible.

## LEGACY-RME-PDF-1C — Controlled Publish & Patient History Integration (2026-08-10)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-pdf-1c-controlled-publish-patient-history` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, 1B merge `6b9ed7aa0e9971ea74adcc69e2e5f5e439c22d11`, GO tag `legacy-rme-pdf-1b-private-upload-rendering-go`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-pdf-1c-controlled-publish-patient-history.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/90-legacy-rme-pdf-archive.mdc` (rules 36–52). Closes the loop 1B stopped short of: a rendered import is reviewed by a human, published into an **immutable** legacy record, and appears in the patient's RME history behind a private read-only viewer. **NO migration** (1A already shipped every column and constraint, incl. `UNIQUE(source_import_id)` and the reviewed/published actor columns), **no seeder, no permission change** (the five 1A permissions are the boundary), no schema change; feature flag `rme.legacy_pdf_archive` stays **OFF** in production so the deploy is inert by construction.

**Flow (1A transition map NOT widened):** `READY_FOR_REVIEW → REVIEWED` (`POST settings/rme/legacy-imports/{import}/review`, `review_legacy_rme_imports`) → `REVIEWED → PUBLISHED` (`POST .../publish`, `publish_legacy_rme_imports`); `PUBLISHED` is terminal (no republish). Published viewer `GET rme/legacy-records/{record}[/source][/pages/{page}]` (`rme.legacy-records.*`, `view_legacy_rme_imports`) — GET-only, its own group under `/rme` (clinical, not Master Data), `permission:` applied INSIDE the `auth` group so a guest gets the login redirect rather than a 403.

**Key gotcha — `Gate::before` means a policy is not a boundary for Super Admin,** who is the only role holding these permissions. Every rule that must hold is enforced AGAIN in the service (status/date/pages/source) or the controller (VOID streaming, feature flag), and the tests assert each one *as a Super Admin*. New `LegacyRmePublishService`: one `DB::transaction` opened with `lockForUpdate`, status re-read under the lock, idempotent via `findBySourceImportId` short-circuit, with `UNIQUE(source_import_id)` as the independent DB defence; 1A date rules **re-validated at publish time** against a freshly resolved cutoff (the intake snapshot is evidence, never authority); refuses an incomplete archive with stable path-free codes (`RENDERED_PAGES_MISSING`/`PAGE_COUNT_MISMATCH`/`PAGE_FILE_MISSING`/`SOURCE_FILE_MISSING`/`IMPORT_NOT_PUBLISHABLE`/`IMPORT_NOT_REVIEWABLE`). **Second gotcha — a refusal audit written INSIDE the failing transaction is rolled back with it**; new `LegacyRmePublishRefusal` carries the field + PII-free metadata out of the transaction and is converted to a `ValidationException` at the boundary (it must never escape as a 500 — that bug was caught in review for the `review()` path too). Publishing **promotes metadata and moves no bytes** (1B's private per-import directory is already final), so a rolled-back publish cannot orphan a file.

**Patient history:** new read-only `LegacyRmePatientHistoryService` merges the caller's already-resolved native visit history with the patient's **PUBLISHED** legacy records into `LegacyRmeTimelineEntry` rows, ordered by the **clinical** date (`rme_date` / `visit_date`, never upload time) with a deterministic tie-break. Legacy and native stay separate entities — publishing creates no `ClinicVisit`/`MedicalRecord`/invoice/payment/consent/odontogram/lab candidate/order/SATUSEHAT candidate and never changes a visit status (asserted by count-before/after tests). The native side is passed in from `ClinicVisitController::show()` rather than re-queried (no second definition of "the patient's visits"); the merged card renders **nothing** when the flag is off, the permission is missing, or the patient has no archive — so the RME workspace is unchanged from before this sprint.

**Security decisions (from an explicit review — no CRITICAL/HIGH found):** a **VOIDed archive stops streaming its bytes** (404 in the controller, not just the policy) because VOID exists for a mis-filed archive, canonically one attached to the WRONG patient; the row stays readable (retracted, not erased). `LegacyRmeImportPageStatus::VIEWABLE` is deliberately wider than `PUBLISHABLE` so a published import's staging pages stay viewable as review evidence. Streaming honours the **disk stored on the row** (`diskFor()` re-asserts it is private) so repointing the configured disk cannot silently 404 history. `PublishLegacyRmeImportRequest::prepareForValidation()` 404s when the flag is off so a malformed payload cannot answer 422 and confirm the endpoint exists. Gallery thumbnails are not audited (opening the archive already writes `RECORD_VIEWED`). Review is a mandatory **state** gate, **not** four-eyes — `publish()` deliberately allows the same actor, since only Super Admin holds either permission; enforce separation of duties in the service and split the roles if that changes.

**Tests:** new `LegacyRmePublishTest`, `LegacyRmePatientHistoryTest`, `LegacyRmeRecordViewerTest`. Repinned two superseded 1B assertions (same pattern every prior sprint used): `LegacyRmeNonRegressionTest` "no review/publish endpoint" → "exactly the staging surface plus review and publish" (+ a new GET-only assertion for the viewer), and `LegacyRmeImportAuthorizationTest` "publish route does not exist" → "no publish action offered on an unreviewed import". **PostgreSQL gotcha:** a unique violation aborts the surrounding `RefreshDatabase` transaction (`SQLSTATE[25P02]`), so the constraint assertion runs inside a nested transaction (SAVEPOINT) and behaves identically on SQLite and PG. Green: LegacyRme 237 (SQLite **and** PostgreSQL 16.14, matching production), `tests/Feature/RME` 1031, RME critical regressions, real Poppler executes (never silently skips), pint repo-wide + `git diff --check` clean. Deploy: no migration/seed; keep `FEATURE_RME_LEGACY_PDF_ARCHIVE=false`; rollback `scripts/rollback-vps.sh legacy-rme-pdf-1b-private-upload-rendering-go`.

**Governance metadata closure (post-merge, inert):** the `rme.legacy_pdf_archive` entry in `config/feature_flags.php` still described the 1A foundation — description "upload/publish runtime lands in a follow-up sprint", `rollout_status: foundation`, `review_target: LEGACY-RME-PDF-1B`, and a rollback action claiming "nothing is imported or published". All four statements were falsified by shipped 1B and 1C runtime, so the flag was realigned to `rollout_status: implemented` / `review_target: LEGACY-RME-PDF-1C`, a description covering 1A+1B+1C, and a rollback action stating that data **persists** (schema, staging rows, published records and their private files) and only the runtime becomes inaccessible. Same convention as `satusehat.integration_readiness` / `satusehat.external_submission_enabled`: **`implemented` marks that the runtime exists, never that production exposure is on** — `default` stays `false`, `risk_level` stays `high`, `env_key` is unchanged, and `rollout_status` is free-form metadata (no enum, no validator) consumed only by `FeatureFlagService::hydrate()`, `foundation:feature-flags` and `PostgresRuntimeGovernanceService` reporting. **Known trap, deliberately NOT changed here:** this flag has no `env_value => env(...)` capture, so under a config-cached deploy a runtime `FEATURE_RME_LEGACY_PDF_ARCHIVE=true` is ignored and the flag resolves to its `false` default — fail-closed today, but the SATUSEHAT flags show that capture is required before any controlled-rollout sprint can actually enable this in production.

## LEGACY-RME-PDF-ROLL-1 — Feature Flag Runtime Override & Controlled Enablement Readiness (2026-08-10)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-pdf-roll-1-feature-flag-runtime-readiness` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline 1C GO `cee66f7515fa96fc8b6e47832db872c55fe92b58`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-pdf-roll-1-feature-flag-runtime-readiness.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/92-feature-flag-runtime-override.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (FOUNDATION_SPRINT). Closes the carry-forward trap the 1C closure recorded verbatim. **Enables nothing — `rme.legacy_pdf_archive` stays `false` in production; no migration, no route, no permission, no schema, no business-workflow change.**

**Root cause — a shared registry defect, NOT a Legacy RME one.** `FeatureFlagService::hydrate()` was already correct (captured `env_value` → runtime `env()` → default). The registry was not: `env_value` capture was a hand-written per-flag opt-in. On the canonical base **all 28 flags declared an `env_key` but only 3 captured it** (`lab.workflow_v2`, `satusehat.integration_readiness`, `satusehat.external_submission_enabled`), leaving **25 inert under `config:cache`**. Laravel's `LoadEnvironmentVariables` returns early when config is cached, so `env()` reads nothing and the declared default silently wins — and **production runs cached** (`bootstrap/cache/config.php` present on the VPS). The documented `env_key` was therefore a promise the system did not keep for 25 flags. Fixed once, canonically; **no Legacy-RME-only env parser was added** (that would have created a second flag system).

**Changes.** (1) `config/feature_flags.php` applies capture **systematically** in one loop at the bottom of the file to every flag declaring an `env_key` — a new flag cannot forget it; the result stays `var_export()`-safe for `config:cache`; an explicit `env_value` still wins, and the 3 existing explicit captures are left untouched so the diff on `lab.workflow_v2` (the only production-enabled flag) is **zero**. (2) New `FeatureFlagService::resolveOverride()` is the single decision point: unset → default; `true/1/on/yes` → on; `false/0/off/no` → off; **blank/whitespace → default** (deliberately NOT `false`, so a default-true safety flag keeps its gate ON); **unparseable → declared default** + reported `invalid` (so a typo can never enable a risky default-off flag *nor* disable a default-true safety flag). (3) Hydrated output gains `env_captured`, `env_resolution` (`default|env|invalid_fallback_default`) and a **normalized** `env_value` (`true|false|null|'invalid'`) — the raw environment string is **never** echoed into evidence JSON. (4) Two governance checks make the gap unreintroducible: **`FLAG-ENV-CAPTURE`** FAILS when a flag declares an `env_key` without a capture, **`FLAG-ENV-VALUE-VALID`** FAILS on an unparseable override. (5) `foundation:feature-flags` prints `via=` and `captured=` per flag for rollout debugging. (6) `LegacyRmeFeatureGuard` **unchanged** — it already delegated to `FeatureFlagService`; it only gains tests proving it inherits the canonical resolution.

**Production impact provably nil:** the VPS `.env` sets exactly one feature variable, `FEATURE_LAB_WORKFLOW_V2=true`, which already had capture and already resolved `true`. Every other variable is unset → capture stores `null` → each flag keeps its declared default. No effective flag value changes on this deploy.

**Verification** uses a genuine cached-config round trip (build the registry with a controlled environment → `var_export` to a file → require it back → resolve with the environment removed), not an uncached local run. Matrix: cached+`true` **off→ON (the defect, fixed)**; cached+unset/blank/`false`/invalid → off; invalid also → governance FAIL; `lab.workflow_v2` stays ON. Tests: `tests/Feature/Foundation/FeatureFlagRuntimeOverrideTest.php` (23) — capture contract, governance FAIL on a removed capture, resolution matrix, invalid redaction, deterministic rollback, guard inheritance, request-input rejection, whole-registry no-drift sweep. **Test-infra gotcha:** these tests must never depend on ambient process environment — `ffBuildRegistry()` snapshots and restores every variable it touches, and the no-drift sweep builds from an explicitly cleared environment; an earlier version that read ambient `config()` failed only when run after a sibling test.

**Durable rules (mirror rule 92):** one registry + one resolver; dotted flag keys never via `config()` dot traversal; a declared `env_key` MUST be captured at config-build time; an uncaptured `env_key` FAILS governance; unset/blank/invalid all fall back to the declared default and invalid FAILS governance; never echo the raw override; **server-side only** (no request/query/header/session/browser input may influence a flag); risky flags default OFF and enabling one is a WATCH never a silent GO; rollback is `false` + rebuild cache — never a schema rollback, never deletion; **code readiness ≠ production rollout** (a GO tag ships the capability *disabled*; `rollout_status: implemented` describes the code, not the production switch).

## LEGACY-RME-PDF-1D — VOID & Clinical Read/Print Completion (2026-08-11)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-pdf-1d-void-clinical-read-print` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline ROLL-1 GO `9e90c7bb2bf59fc4ddd966df6dfcc8b52b9c8e4b`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-pdf-1d-void-clinical-read-print.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/93-legacy-rme-void-clinical-read.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (MODULE_SPRINT). **Production stays OFF — `rme.legacy_pdf_archive` remains `false` and every 1D route answers 404 server-side while it is. NO migration** (`voided_by`/`voided_at`/`void_reason` exist since 1A, `PUBLISHED → VOID` was already terminal, `LegacyRmeRecordPolicy::void()` and `void_legacy_rme_imports` already existed — 1D supplies the runtime they waited for).

**VOID.** New `LegacyRmeVoidService` + `VoidLegacyRmeRecordRequest` + `POST rme/legacy-records/{record}/void` (`rme.legacy-records.void`, `permission:void_legacy_rme_imports`). **Retracts, never erases**: row, pages and private files all survive; `markVoided()` writes ONLY status/voided_by/voided_at/void_reason (hard-coded column list in the repository) so a "correction" can never be smuggled in as a void. **Terminal** — no un-void, no republish, no delete; correction is VOID + a **fresh import**. **Reason mandatory** (≥10 chars) enforced in the FormRequest *and again* in the service. **Atomic/idempotent** — one transaction opening with `lockForUpdate` re-reading status; a second void is a no-op preserving the FIRST reason/actor and writes no second audit row. **Audit** `LEGACY_RME_VOIDED` carries `status` + new allow-listed `void_reason_length` only — the free-text reason is NEVER in the payload (it may name a patient; its home is the `void_reason` column). A VOIDed archive stops streaming source/pages/print/export but stays readable as evidence.

**Repository exception (deliberate):** `LegacyRmeRecordRepositoryInterface` still exposes no generic `update()`/`delete()`. 1D adds exactly two narrow named methods — `lockForUpdate()` + `markVoided()` — because a generic update would quietly restore in-place mutation of clinical evidence.

**Clinical reader.** New permission **`view_legacy_rme_archive`** (PermissionSeeder) granted to **Doctor** (RoleSeeder). `view_legacy_rme_imports` is the INTAKE tier (Super Admin); granting it to a doctor merely to read would hand out archive management. New `LegacyRmeRecordPolicy::READ_PERMISSIONS` accepts either; route group is `permission:view_legacy_rme_imports|view_legacy_rme_archive`; `LegacyRmePatientHistoryService` uses the same constant so timeline and viewer can never disagree. **It is deliberately NOT in `LegacyRmeWorkspaceScope::GOVERNANCE_PERMISSIONS`** — otherwise a doctor would widen from their own branch to every RME branch. Out-of-scope stays **404, never 403**. **Honest correction:** "fail closed on no branch" is NOT accurate — `BranchContext` has a documented fallback chain (online context → `users.branch_id` → relation → MAIN/first-active), so an unpinned doctor still resolves *some* branch; the property that actually holds (and is tested) is **non-widening** (scope ≤ 1 branch, never the governance set, provenance-less rows governance-only).

**Print/export.** `GET {record}/print` (browser `window.print()`) + `GET {record}/export` (dompdf download). Both behind auth + permission + policy + feature flag, both refuse a VOIDed record. **Print REFERENCES pages through the policy-gated page route** (browser re-requests each with the caller's own session) so printing is never a weaker door; **export embeds pages as data URIs read through the storage abstraction** because dompdf carries no session — the absolute path stays inside the storage service. Both outputs are visibly "ARSIP RME LAMA — DOKUMEN HISTORIS (HANYA BACA)"; dompdf template is table-based (no flexbox/external asset); download filename generic (no patient name/RM number); KTP/NIK nowhere — asserted at runtime AND by a static template scan. Export bounded at `MAX_EXPORT_PAGES = 30` (says so when truncated, points at the source PDF) to avoid an OOM from inlining dozens of full-resolution PNGs. **GD gotcha:** dompdf decodes the embedded PNGs through GD; local CLI has none, CI (`gd, exif`) and production (`true`) do — the 2 tests that genuinely render are GD-guarded (same convention as `LabWorkflowUploadCompressionTest`) and were verified in `serversideup/php:8.3-cli` + `install-php-extensions gd` → **28/28 passed with GD**, so the export path is proven not merely skipped.

**Superseded assertions repinned (same pattern 1C used for 1B):** (1) `LegacyRmeNonRegressionTest` "read-only surface with no write route" → "plus exactly one retraction route" — intent preserved, not relaxed: VOID is the only write, it is a **POST** (never PUT/PATCH/DELETE, which would imply edit/erasure), every other route stays GET/HEAD, and no `update`/`destroy`/`republish` route exists. (2) `LegacyRmeRecordViewerTest` "rejects a role that holds no legacy archive permission" no longer asserts 403 for **Doctor** (now a legitimate clinical reader bounded by branch scope, asserted separately) and instead runs over Kasir/Perawat/Admin Lab.

Tests: `LegacyRmeVoidTest` (18), `LegacyRmeClinicalReadPrintTest` (28). Green: **LegacyRme 286 passed / 2 GD-skipped**.

**Next:** controlled production enablement of `rme.legacy_pdf_archive` remains a separate, explicit rollout decision — 1D completes the capability, it does not turn it on.

## LEGACY-RME-PDF-ROLL-2 — Controlled Pilot Enablement & Operational Readiness (2026-08-11)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-pdf-roll-2-controlled-pilot-enablement` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ 1D merge `61806ff`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-pdf-roll-2-controlled-pilot-enablement.md`; runbook `docs/runbooks/legacy-rme-pdf-rollout-runbook.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/94-legacy-rme-controlled-rollout.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml`. **No migration, no permission, no route, no schema change; no new clinical capability.** 1A–1D delivered the archive runtime and ROLL-1 made the flag's runtime override survive `config:cache`; ROLL-2 answers the operational question none of them did — **may this deployment switch the archive ON, and can it be switched back OFF?**

**What shipped:** `config/legacy_rme_rollout.php` (the rollout contract: 4 required tables, 6 permissions, 17 route names split operator/clinical, Poppler binaries, queue posture, private-disk posture, rollback contract, pilot-scope gate); read-only `App\Modules\LegacyRme\Services\LegacyRmeRolloutReadinessService` (13 checks, each individually guarded — a throwing dependency degrades to UNKNOWN rather than taking the gate down) + immutable `Support\LegacyRmeRolloutCheck`; command **`php artisan legacy-rme:rollout-readiness`** (`--json`, `--strict`, `--expect=off|on`). Reuses `FeatureFlagService`, `LegacyRmeFeatureGuard` and `BranchService` — no parallel flag resolution, no re-derived RME branch set, no repository or model added.

**Decision model:** `FAIL` **and `UNKNOWN` both yield `NO_GO`** — "we could not evaluate it" is never a basis for switching a clinical feature on. WATCH exits 0 by default, non-zero under `--strict`. The gate is read-only: it never enables the feature, never writes a clinical row, never repairs what it finds; the only write is a self-cleaning storage probe.

**Key checks:** `runtime_override_capture` is the ROLL-1 regression guard — a declared override key not captured at config-BUILD time is silently ignored under a cached config, which breaks **enablement and rollback equally**, so it fails closed. `effective_state` proves the switch in both directions via `--expect` (an override that is set but does not resolve means the OFF path is broken too → stop). `queue_contract` blocks a `sync` connection in a worker-expecting environment, because rasterization is queued by design and `sync` would drag a multi-minute render into the operator's upload request. `pilot_scope_approved` is the decisive gate.

**Metadata correction (real drift, caught by hand then pinned by the gate):** the flag's `review_target` still read `LEGACY-RME-PDF-1C` after 1D shipped and the description never mentioned VOID / doctor viewer / export. Both corrected to the delivered runtime. This is the **same drift class that already forced a follow-up PR during 1C**, so `flag_metadata_current` now compares the registry `review_target` against `legacy_rme_rollout.delivered_sprint` and reports WATCH on divergence — the rot is caught by a command instead of by a reader.

**Pilot scope approval (never inferred):** `LEGACY_RME_PILOT_APPROVED` + `LEGACY_RME_PILOT_APPROVAL_REFERENCE` (non-PHI governance reference) + `LEGACY_RME_PILOT_BRANCH_CODE` (single branch; `MAIN` refused; must be active **and** RME-enabled on this deployment). **Deliberately NOT stored anywhere in config: patient identifier, document path, any name** — patient ownership and the historical-date rule are enforced per import by the 1A date-rule service and revalidated at 1C publish, which is where a per-patient decision belongs. Shipping code, a GO tag, an existing Super Admin, or a prior passing sprint are **never** authorization.

**Durable rules (rule 94):** enablement is an explicit recorded human decision, never a deploy side effect; OFF is the resting state unless sustained enablement was explicitly authorized; the flag is read only through `LegacyRmeFeatureGuard`→`FeatureFlagService` (dotted `config()` traversal returns null); enablement and rollback are proven with `--expect`, not assumed; never invent pilot clinical data; the document date comes from the document (never upload time/`created_at`/today); operator vs doctor role separation holds during a pilot (never widen a permission to make a step pass); correction is **VOID + fresh import**, never editing published evidence; **rollback preserves everything** (schema, staging rows, published/voided records, private files) and never touches native RME; a legacy action never creates a native artifact (verify by count comparison, not assumption); evidence carries no PHI; never enable by editing the server or hand-patching the cached config file; **a green pilot authorizes nothing beyond its scope**.

**RESUMED for the clinical pilot (2026-08-12).** ROLL-2 was PAUSED before the pilot pending **LEGACY-RME-PDF-FIX-ROLL2-1**; that corrective is GO-tagged (`legacy-rme-pdf-fix-roll2-1-eligibility-multidate-rm-branch-go` @ `f37301115226b4f20710e247a51c51c1980662b1`) and is an ancestor of the pilot base, so ROLL-2 is resumed. **Durable additions to rule 94 (items 16–21):** (16) ROLL-2 requires the FIX-ROLL2-1 GO in its base. (17) **The approved pilot branch must match the RM-derived branch** — `LEGACY_RME_PILOT_BRANCH_CODE` records the owner's approval while the landing branch is derived from the patient's Nomor RM and cannot be overridden; if they disagree the gate approves one branch while imports land in another, so correct the *approval*, never the derivation. For the `DG-TKM1-…` pilot the approved scope is **`TKM1` / Cabang Telkomas**; the earlier **`LDK2` scope is superseded for this pilot** (it was set on the VPS before the corrective existed). (18) A pilot patient with **no native RME is valid** (`NO_NATIVE_REFERENCE`) — never create a visit or medical record to make an import eligible. (19) Multi-date pilot documents use the **earliest** clinical date as `selected_rme_date` and the **latest** as `latest_rme_date`; publish re-validates the whole range against a freshly resolved native boundary. (20) **There is no OCR** — the system cannot detect an under-declared latest date, so the reviewer must visually confirm it against the document; never claim OCR enforcement in pilot evidence. (21) The sidebar entry **"Impor Arsip RME Lama"** (Master Data) is **feature-gated as well as permission-gated** (`@canany([view/create_legacy_rme_imports])` + `LegacyRmeFeatureGuard::enabled()`), so it appears on enablement and disappears on rollback — a convenience, never the security boundary, since the routes fail closed independently. **Pilot scope:** RM `DG-TKM1-2024-9985` → branch `TKM1`, no native RME, PDF dates 28-01-2024 + 31-08-2024 → `selected_rme_date` 28-01-2024, `latest_rme_date` 31-08-2024.

**PILOT DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED (rule 94 items 22–23).** The first real pilot upload staged correctly (import id 1, patient 36, branch TKM1, 28-01-2024/31-08-2024, private disk, 632031 bytes) but then sat at **`QUEUED` with `page_count=NULL` forever**: `ProcessLegacyRmePdfImport` dispatches to the dedicated queue **`legacy-rme-documents`** (`config/legacy_rme.php` `processing.queue`), while the POST-ENT systemd worker consumed only `default,reports,notifications,maintenance`. **No failed job, no error, no log line** — the pipeline was structurally incapable of rendering, and `legacy-rme:rollout-readiness` still reported `queue_contract` **GO** because it only checked that the *connection* was not `sync`. Fix: (a) `legacy-rme-documents` added to `queue_governance.ent5_retry_failed_job.allowed_queue_names`, (b) added to `deploy/systemd/daengtisiams-queue-worker.service` `--queue=`, (c) `queue_contract` now reads the tracked worker unit (`legacy_rme_rollout.queue.worker_unit_file`) and **fails closed** unless it actually consumes the rendering queue (exact comma-split match, so `legacy-rme-doc` never matches `legacy-rme-documents`) and that queue is an approved ENT-5 name; an unreadable unit is also a FAIL. **The three sides — job queue name, ENT-5 allowed list, worker `--queue=` — must be changed together.** A stuck import is never hand-edited forward. Tests: 6 new in `LegacyRmeRolloutReadinessTest` (note: pin `queue.default=database` in such tests or the pre-existing `sync` branch short-circuits before the new logic).

**AUTHORIZATION DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED (rule 94 items 24–27).** The pilot's doctor check exposed a second, security-relevant defect: `LegacyRmeRecordPolicy::view` and `LegacyRmePatientHistoryService::publishedRecordsFor` were scoped by **branch only**, while native RME scopes a doctor by **clinical relationship** (`DoctorPatientScopeService`: an active `trx_rme_patient_doctor_assignments` row, or a `trx_clinic_visits` row with that doctor). Branch is wider, so a same-branch doctor with **no relationship to the patient** was refused the native record yet could open that patient's **legacy archive** (production: doctor 9, whose patient scope held 1 patient not including 36, got HTTP 200 on `/rme/legacy-records/1`). Fix: both surfaces now additionally require `DoctorPatientScopeService::doctorCanAccessPatient` — the canonical native service **reused verbatim, never re-implemented** (a parallel legacy scope would drift and drift always fails open). Governing rule: **Legacy RME must never widen clinical visibility compared with native RME.** Non-doctor actors unaffected (intake/governance keep branch scope + named permissions, so import/review/publish/VOID are untouched); Super Admin unaffected via `Gate::before`. Response codes differ meaningfully and are pinned: same-branch-but-not-treating → **403** (the branch-scoped repository finds the row, the policy refuses it), wrong-branch → **404** (the scope never surfaces it). **Consequence for legacy-only patients:** a migrated patient with 0 visits has no clinical relationship, so no doctor can discover or open their archive — the correct negative result, never repaired by manufacturing a visit/assignment/branch pin/permission. **Test-fixture change:** 1D doctor fixtures created a doctor *user* with no linked `mst_doctors` row, which passed only under branch-only scope; `lrme1dTreatingDoctor()` now links a real `Doctor` + `PatientDoctorAssignment`. Tests: A–E matrix in `LegacyRmeClinicalReadPrintTest` (same-branch-not-treating 403, wrong-branch 404, treating+owning-branch 200, visit-as-relationship, read-only-only, no `Gate::before` widening, timeline hidden from non-treating doctor).

**PILOT COMPLETE — feature returned to OFF (2026-08-12).** Two full lifecycles ran on the VPS against RM `DG-TKM1-2024-9985` (patient 36, branch TKM1 derived from the RM, **no native RME** = `NO_NATIVE_REFERENCE`, dates 28-01-2024 earliest / 31-08-2024 latest). **Lifecycle 1:** operator web upload → import 1 stuck at `QUEUED` (defect 1) → after the queue fix + unit re-install the *existing* job 2 was consumed **naturally in ~18 s** (no re-upload, no redispatch, **no state surgery**) → `READY_FOR_REVIEW` pages=1 (real Poppler, 1898×2500 @180dpi, private disk) → reviewed → **PUBLISHED** (record 1) → **VOID** (reason 70 chars; pages + source retained; row 200, bytes 404). **Lifecycle 2 (fresh correction):** the **same approved PDF** re-uploaded — canonically permitted because `LegacyRmeDuplicateDetectionService` does not block on a VOID record's checksum (`DUP blocked=false`, identical `sha=bc1ab8cf…`, **bytes never modified**) → rendered in 6 s → reviewed → **PUBLISHED** (record 2). Final state: record 1 `VOID` / record 2 `PUBLISHED`, both retaining provenance (`source_import_id` 1 and 2); patient history shows **exactly 1 active entry** (id 2, representative date 2024-01-28). **Zero unintended native artifacts** in both lifecycles across visits/medical records/invoices/payments/odontograms/lab orders/lab candidates/SATUSEHAT/failed_jobs. Audit trail complete (`IMPORT_CREATED×2, PDF_UPLOADED×2, PROCESSING_QUEUED×2, PROCESSING_COMPLETED×2, IMPORT_REVIEWED×2, PUBLISHED×2, VOIDED×1` + read/print/export) and **PHI-clean** (name/RM/KTP/storage-path all absent). **Rollback:** `enabled=false, env_resolution=env, env_captured=true, guard=false`, readiness `--expect=off --strict` **GO exit 0** with `queue_contract` still healthy; **authenticated Super Admin with valid CSRF gets 404** on index/create/show/record/print/export/source and on POST publish + POST void (**not** a bare 419); the sidebar entry disappears; legacy rows **and** private files all preserved; native RME untouched (26 visits / 26 records, routes intact); health 4/4 200. **Store-route nuance:** an *invalid* POST to `settings.rme.legacy-imports.store` returns **302** rather than 404 because Laravel validates the FormRequest before invoking the controller — `assertFeatureEnabled()` is nevertheless the first statement of `store()`, so no import can be created while OFF (verified: counts unchanged). **Doctor read-only:** production **negative** authorization PASS (patient 36 has no clinical relationship → 403 on all five surfaces, 0 history entries); production **positive** doctor read **NOT APPLICABLE** — no legitimate existing doctor-patient case, and none was manufactured; positive contract proven by the automated A–E matrix. **Log delta:** the only ERROR lines are 3 self-inflicted `psysh`/`HOME` tinker-probe entries (operator probe, not pilot or deploy). **Deploy gotcha for `runuser -u www-data … artisan tinker`:** psysh needs a writable HOME — use `env HOME=/tmp`, or every tinker call fails with *"Writing to directory /var/www/.config/psysh is not allowed"* and logs an ERROR.

Tests: `tests/Feature/LegacyRme/LegacyRmeRolloutReadinessTest.php` (22 passed / 62 assertions) — fail-closed on unapproved/incomplete/`MAIN`/non-RME scope, publicly-exposable and undefined disk, `sync` queue, uncaptured override; `--expect` both directions; probe leaves nothing behind; report free of patient identifiers and 16-digit runs; UNKNOWN blocks. Deploy note: **no migration/seed needed**; post-deploy verify `runuser -u www-data -- php artisan legacy-rme:rollout-readiness --expect=off --strict`. **ROLL-2 GO does not authorize** all-branch rollout, bulk/background historical import, permanent enablement, bypassing review, converting the archive into native RME, or SATUSEHAT submission of legacy data — widening is a separate approved stage.

## LEGACY-RME-PDF-FIX-ROLL2-1 — Legacy Eligibility, Multi-Date & RM-Branch Resolution (2026-08-12)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-pdf-fix-roll2-1-eligibility-multidate-rm-branch` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ ROLL-2 merge `382aaf100714414527ad499ec832acc9d39484f8`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-pdf-fix-roll2-1-eligibility-multidate-rm-branch.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/95-legacy-rme-migration-eligibility-rm-branch.mdc` (rule 90 items 6/7/12 corrected in place — **where they disagree, rule 95 wins**). **Corrective sprint: ROLL-2 stays PAUSED; a FIX GO unblocks ROLL-2 but is NOT a pilot GO.** Production `rme.legacy_pdf_archive` stays **OFF** through FIX closure; no pilot PDF uploaded; patient 36 not mutated; **no ROLL-2 GO tag created**.

**Root cause (found preparing the real pilot doc for RM `DG-TKM1-2024-9985`, patient 36 — 0 native visits, 0 native medical records):** (1) `LegacyRmeDateRuleService` refused any patient with no native RME (`PATIENT_HAS_NO_NATIVE_RME`), which made the ORDINARY migration case impossible and pushed the operator toward manufacturing a clinical encounter — the native RME is a **BOUND, not a PREREQUISITE**; (2) a document was treated as one date although a real PDF carries several (pilot doc: 28-01-2024 + 31-08-2024), so operators had no rule for which to enter, and validating one chosen date let a document whose OLDEST entry predates the native RME but whose NEWEST does not slip through, hiding mixed chronology behind its oldest date; (3) `origin_branch_id` was a free `<select>` labelled *"Opsional — hanya catatan asal arsip"*, but `LegacyRmeImportRepository::scoped()` filters row visibility on it and `LegacyRmeImportPolicy::inScope()` evaluates it — the service also trusted a submitted id and anchored a blank one to the uploader's first branch, either of which files a patient's history under a branch that does not own them, while the answer was already in the patient's own RM.

**Product decisions (permanent):** (1) **no native RME is VALID** — never a hidden exception, never manufacture a visit/medical record to satisfy a date rule; (2) native RME, when present, bounds the archive; (3) multi-date document → `selected_rme_date` = **EARLIEST** clinical date (representative, drives patient history); (4) safety bound → **`MAX(document dates) < earliest native RME`** (validating only the representative date is unsafe); (5) single-date document → range collapses, latest may be omitted; (6) branch = branch code in the patient's canonical Nomor RM (`DG-TKM1-2024-9985` → `TKM1` → Cabang Telkomas); (7) operator **cannot** override it — a conflicting `origin_branch_id` is **rejected explicitly** (`BRANCH_CONFLICT`), never silently discarded; (8) missing/malformed/unknown/ambiguous/inactive/non-RME/out-of-scope branch → **fail closed, no fallback** (not the actor's branch, not `BranchContext`, not the first RME branch, never a request value); (9) `origin_branch_id` is **security/visibility scope**, not descriptive metadata. **No date OCR was invented** — both ends stay operator-declared; the UI states the earliest-date rule and the server enforces ordering + native + today + birth-date bounds.

**Architecture.** New `App\Modules\Patient\Support\MedicalRecordNumberParts` + **`PatientMedicalRecordNumberService::parse()`/`branchCodeFrom()`** — the parser lives beside the Sprint 23.8 composer so the two cannot drift; `parse($x)?->toString() === $x` for every composable value (incl. a manual sequence containing a hyphen); anything non-canonical returns `null`, never a partial guess. No controller/request/service/view carries a regex for this format. New `LegacyRmeBranchResolver` + `LegacyRmeBranchResolution` (codes `RM_MISSING`, `INVALID_RM_BRANCH_CODE`, `BRANCH_NOT_FOUND`, `BRANCH_AMBIGUOUS`, `BRANCH_INACTIVE`, `BRANCH_NOT_RME_ENABLED`, `BRANCH_OUT_OF_SCOPE`, `BRANCH_CONFLICT`) reusing `BranchService::rmeEnabledIds()` (MAIN excluded by definition) + `LegacyRmeWorkspaceScope`. **`BRANCH_AMBIGUOUS` is defence-in-depth and unreachable** — `mst_branches.code` is UNIQUE; the test asserts the DB refuses a duplicate rather than faking data the schema forbids. `LegacyRmeDateRuleService::evaluate(Patient, $selected, $latest = null)` validates the whole range; `PATIENT_HAS_NO_NATIVE_RME` **removed** (keeping a FAIL code while allowing the import would be misleading) and replaced by evidence-only reference modes **`BEFORE_NATIVE_RME` / `NO_NATIVE_REFERENCE`**; new failure code `LEGACY_DATE_RANGE_INVALID`; a **blank** latest date = single-date document, a **non-blank unparseable** one is an error that must not collapse onto the earliest date (that would drop the bound). `LegacyRmePublishService` re-validates the whole persisted range against a FRESH cutoff **and** re-derives the branch (a stale owner must not be frozen into an immutable record); the actor is deliberately not passed to the resolver there (that is the policy's job); a pre-FIX `origin_branch_id = NULL` row is left alone rather than guessed at.

**Migration (additive only — `migrate`, never `migrate:fresh`/`db:wipe`):** `2026_08_11_100001_add_latest_rme_date_to_legacy_rme_tables` adds nullable `latest_rme_date` to `stg_rme_legacy_imports` + `trx_rme_legacy_records`. **Persistence is required, not optional:** publish re-validates against a fresh cutoff and cannot re-check a range it does not have — staging-only validation would leave publish-time revalidation blind to exactly the race it exists to catch. Existing rows keep NULL = "single-date document", the same meaning they already had (production holds 0 legacy imports / 0 legacy records, but the change is safe on non-empty data).

**Audit.** Allow-list gains `latest_rme_date`, `reference_mode`, `branch_code` (operational label like `TKM1`, never the full Nomor RM). New action **`LEGACY_RME_IMPORT_BRANCH_REJECTED`** — a distinct action, not a reused `IMPORT_CREATED`, because nothing was created and a trail saying otherwise is a lie.

**UI.** The blocking "Pasien belum memiliki RME di sistem" **danger** alert → **info** notice ("normal untuk pasien migrasi… tidak menghalangi impor") that explicitly warns against creating a visit/RM to satisfy the rule; two date inputs (*Tanggal RME Paling Awal* required, *Tanggal RME Paling Akhir* optional); an explicit multi-date instruction to use the earliest date; the branch `<select>` **removed** in favour of a read-only display of the derived branch + code, and the misleading *"hanya catatan asal arsip"* copy deleted.

**Test-fixture change.** `PatientFactory` emits a generic `MRN-XXXXXXXX` placeholder that is NOT a canonical Nomor RM; it is **deliberately left alone** (other suites pin it). New Pest helpers `legacyRmeBranch()` + `legacyRmeArchivablePatient()` create a patient with a real `DG-{CODE}-{YEAR}-{SEQ}` number and its branch — the shape production actually has — and the Legacy RME fixtures were migrated to them.

Tests: new `tests/Feature/LegacyRme/LegacyRmeMigrationEligibilityBranchTest.php` (39 — RM parser incl. round-trip + 10 malformed inputs, full branch fail-closed matrix, no-fallback, conflict, PII-free rejection audit, no-native import creating 0 visits/0 MRs, multi-date persistence, 4 publish-revalidation races, side-effect regression for BOTH patient kinds, row visibility both ways, 3 HTTP boundary tests); updated `LegacyRmeDateRuleServiceTest`, `LegacyRmePdfValidationTest`, `LegacyRmeImportAuthorizationTest` (the 3 tests pinning operator-chosen / actor-anchored / branchless behaviour replaced). Green: LegacyRme 361 passed / 2 skipped (pre-existing GD skips), RME+Patient+MasterData 1135 passed.

## LEGACY-RME-PDF-ROLL-3 — Multi-Branch Controlled Migration Rollout (2026-08-12)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-pdf-roll-3-multi-branch-controlled-migration` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`; do NOT target main). Immutable prerequisite: `legacy-rme-pdf-roll-2-controlled-pilot-enablement-go` @ `3e9a06d92f6e4c9a3cc22ace46e0a759a4b75e77` (verified `type=tag`, peels to that SHA, ancestor of base) — **never reopened, moved or re-cut**. Sprint doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-pdf-roll-3-multi-branch-controlled-migration.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/96-legacy-rme-multi-branch-controlled-rollout.mdc`; runbook `docs/runbooks/legacy-rme-pdf-rollout-runbook.md` §8b. **No migration, no new route, no new permission, no schema change.**

**The defect closed.** ROLL-2 declared its pilot branch as a single scalar `legacy_rme_rollout.pilot_scope.branch_code`, and a repo-wide search proved it was read by **exactly one consumer — `LegacyRmeRolloutReadinessService`, a report**. `LegacyRmeImportService::createFromUpload()` enforced only the **global** `LegacyRmeFeatureGuard::assertEnabled()` plus the FIX-ROLL2-1 RM-derived branch. So with the capability ON, **every active RME-enabled branch could import**; single-branch confinement was operator discipline, not a server guarantee.

**Architecture — two separate gates, both required.** *CAPABILITY* = flag `rme.legacy_pdf_archive` (whole runtime). *ADMISSION* = `legacy_rme_rollout.admission` via new `App\Modules\LegacyRme\Services\LegacyRmeBranchAdmissionService`. Capability ON + empty allowlist migrates nothing (documented fail-closed default). Admission always decides on the **RM-derived** branch (`DG-TKM1-2024-9985 → TKM1`); `decide()` accepts only a resolved `LegacyRmeBranchResolution`, so no request body/query/session/`BranchContext`/operator branch can influence it. Stable codes on new `LegacyRmeAdmissionDecision`: `ADMITTED`, `FEATURE_DISABLED`, `NO_BRANCH_ADMITTED`, `BRANCH_NOT_ADMITTED`, `BRANCH_FORBIDDEN` (MAIN), `BRANCH_UNRESOLVED`.

**Storage = config/env allowlist, ROLL-1 capture pattern.** `env()` is read ONLY while `config/legacy_rme_rollout.php` is built, so the value bakes into the cached array and survives `config:cache`; a test asserts the service source contains no `env(` call. `LEGACY_RME_ADMITTED_BRANCH_CODES` is normalized once at config-build time into canonical upper-case tokens. **Matching is EXACT-TOKEN**: `TKM` never admits `TKM1`, `TKM1` never admits `TKM1-EXTRA`, and vice versa (case is normalized, not loosened). Partial/prefix/substring matching is forbidden. Also `LEGACY_RME_WAVE` (non-PHI label) and `LEGACY_RME_ADMISSION_ENFORCED` (default true; enforcement OFF in a `worker_required_environments` deployment is a readiness **FAIL**, not a warning).

**NORMAL DRAIN vs EMERGENCY STOP — two distinct operational controls, never interchangeable.** *DRAIN* (remove a branch code + rebuild config cache) stops **new ingestion only** — upload/create and retry re-queue — for that branch; imports already staged and human-reviewed keep their lifecycle and may still be published, with publish running the full canonical revalidation (permission, patient, RM-derived branch, date range, native boundary, state machine). Nothing is deleted, no queued job is corrupted. *EMERGENCY STOP* (capability flag OFF) withdraws **everything including publish**; the operator surface 404s and the decision reports `FEATURE_DISABLED` rather than a wave code so evidence distinguishes an incident stop from a routine rollback. **Draining is never incident containment.**

**Ingestion backpressure** (new `LegacyRmeIngestionCapacityService` + `LegacyRmeIngestionCapacity`): admission also closes on queue depth (`CAPACITY_QUEUE_DEPTH`, default 25), oldest-pending age (`CAPACITY_QUEUE_STALLED`, 1800 s) or low free disk (`CAPACITY_DISK_LOW`, 2 GiB); threshold `0` disables a probe. Throttles **new intake only** — nothing in flight is cancelled, re-prioritised or re-queued — and reopens by itself as the queue drains (no latch). An unmeasurable probe (non-`database` queue driver, unreadable disk) reports `null`, never a faked depth of zero.

**Enforcement points.** `createFromUpload()` gates admission + capacity immediately after branch resolution and **before any byte is stored**, so a refusal leaves no staging row, no orphan file and no queued job. `queue($import, $actor, isRetry: true)` re-gates on the row's own `origin_branch_id` (a retry restarts render work). Two new PII-free audit events `LEGACY_RME_IMPORT_ADMISSION_REJECTED` (distinct from `IMPORT_BRANCH_REJECTED`: there the branch could not be *derived* — a data problem; here it derived cleanly and the *rollout* declined it) and `LEGACY_RME_INGESTION_THROTTLED`; new allow-listed keys `wave`, `pending_jobs`.

**Readiness + ops.** `legacy-rme:rollout-readiness` gains `branch_admission` (FAIL on enforcement-off in a real deployment, a declared forbidden branch, or an admitted code that is not an active RME-enabled branch; WATCH when capability is on with nothing admitted; GO for the closed pre-wave state) and `ingestion_capacity` (WATCH when backpressure is off, all thresholds disabled, or currently saturated — saturation is transient and deliberately not a deploy blocker). New read-only `php artisan legacy-rme:wave-status [--json]` reports admitted branches, wave label, pipeline headroom, imports by status/branch, published/void counts and oldest-awaiting-review hours — counts and branch codes only, never a patient name/Nomor RM/KTP/filename/path. It changes nothing; admission is a config change plus a cache rebuild, never a side effect of a status command.

**Test-fixture decision (important).** `legacyRmeBranch()` in `tests/Pest.php` now also **admits** the branch it creates (new helpers `legacyRmeAdmitBranch()` / `legacyRmeAdmittedBranches()`), mirroring production where an operator admits exactly the branch being migrated. Admission enforcement therefore stays **ON for the entire suite**; disabling it in `phpunit.xml` was rejected because it would leave every ingestion test silently bypassing the gate — which is precisely how ROLL-2 shipped an advisory pilot scope. **Ordering footgun:** a fixture built *after* the allowlist is narrowed silently re-admits itself, so denial tests pass an explicit override applied post-fixture (`roll3ReadyImport(..., ['TKM1'])`). Cross-patient identical PDF bytes trip the real 1B duplicate guard, so multi-branch fixtures must generate structurally distinct documents.

Tests: new `LegacyRmeBranchAdmissionTest` (23), `LegacyRmeIngestionCapacityTest` (10), `LegacyRmeMultiBranchRolloutTest` (7). Green: `tests/Feature/LegacyRme` **416 passed / 2 skipped / 1144 assertions on SQLite AND on pinned PostgreSQL 16.14** (matching CI + production); Poppler integration 5 passed against real `pdfinfo`/`pdftoppm` 26.01.0; rollout readiness 30 passed with the 2 new checks; `legacy-rme:wave-status --json` verified on PostgreSQL; pint clean.

**ROLL-3 GO scope.** Authorizes the server-side capability to run a controlled, reversible, branch-by-branch wave with the admitted set named explicitly. It does **NOT** authorize all branches, permanent global capability ON, bulk/unattended migration, automated PDF discovery, OCR date authority, auto-publish, conversion to native RME, billing, SATUSEHAT submission, or any bypass of human document review, branch admission or `DoctorPatientScope`. **Human date verification remains required — there is still no OCR and no automated clinical-date enforcement.** Default state between waves is **capability OFF with no branch admitted**.
